Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `int_sop_2` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
- output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`, `by`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision1 under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.47292 ns on its one clock tm3_clk_v0 (I/O pads not timed) and uses 42 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 2.56155e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 153334, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 2.14961e+13. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in corr: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (mult_9x9_fixed_pt) straight into a flip-flop (the product register), no adder and no LUT; wire 1.73 ns into the multiply, 1.45 out. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in corr (another product register, endpoint lo09773, not path 1's lo09847), is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile straight into a flip-flop, 5.427 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 35 of the 42 tiles.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision1.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — one reduction per correlator instance, 76 reductions in all
BLOCKS: 76
TILES: 76
STAGE_NS: 2.219
MAPPING: one int_sop_2 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12).

  • In corr: clk=clk (tm3_clk_v0), reset=sop_rst (new, = rst & rst_q), mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im}, by={11'b0,in_r_im}, chainin=37'b0; result[15:0] becomes corr_out_tmp (the value corr_out samples when new_data), result[36:16] unused, chainout unconnected.
  • In corr_seq: clk=clk, reset=sop_rst, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0,in_r_im_reg}, chainin=37'b0; result[15:0] becomes corr_out_tmp, chainout unconnected. The four in_*_reg stay in soft logic (they have a clock enable, new_data, which the block has no port for).

PLAN:

ASSUMES: rst (top port, line 20) is held high across at least two consecutive clock edges at power-up. Support: rst reaches only port_bus_2to1_1 (line 259) and port_bus_1to0 (line 310); neither corr (1352-1387) nor corr_seq (1829-1871) has any reset, so nothing in the baseline can clear the block's registers out of X except a reset the design supplies at start-up, and the block's reset is driven by rst & rst_q (high only on the second and later cycles of a reset pulse). The reference testbench holds rst for RESET_CYCLES=4 cycles (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v lines 15, 238-239), so the assumption holds there; the rewrite matches the original on every other cycle regardless, including after every one-, two- and three-cycle mid-run reset the testbench applies (lines 198-206) and the random resets (line 185).

Candidate regions — all four converted. Lines 1374/1375 are the two products of corr, lines 1867/1868 the two products of corr_seq; in each module the two products are summed into corr_out_tmp one stage later, which is exactly ax*ay + bx*by. Nothing is left: these are the only four multiply expressions in the file, so the rewrite removes every mult_9x9_fixed_pt from the design. Partial conversion was rejected: the baseline's slowest stage (5.47292 ns) and its second slowest (5.427 ns) are both corr product registers of this same shape, and there are 152 such stages, so converting only some of them would add DSP tiles while leaving the clock period where it is.

Correctness of the arithmetic. in_l_re, in_l_im, in_r_re, in_r_im are plain input[7:0] (lines 1357-1360, 1834-1837), so both products are unsigned 8x8 and exact in 16 bits; the block zero-extends each operand to its port width and the exact sum is at most 2·(2^8-1)^2 = 130050 < 2^17, so no wrap occurs inside the block and result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg (which wraps mod 2^16) bit for bit.

Visibility. lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the sum (1385, 1869); corr_out_tmp is read only by corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1379, 1856). Nothing else in the file reads them, so all three register banks may go inside the block. corr_out itself stays in soft logic because it has the new_data clock enable and is the module's output. The wires in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg declared at 1363-1366 in corr are unused in the baseline; leave them.

Register stages, before and after.

  • corr: before — operands arrive combinationally from the sh_reg outputs, multiply, then lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (stage 1), then the adder, then corr_out_tmp (stage 2): 2 stages from operand to result. After — operands into the block's input registers (stage 1), multiply and add combinationally, result register (stage 2): 2 stages. Same latency; the register moves from after the multiply to before it (this retiming is why the tier is T2, not T1).
  • corr_seq: before — in_*_reg (clock-enabled, kept), multiply, lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (stage 1), adder, corr_out_tmp (stage 2): 2 stages from in_*_reg to result. After — in_*_reg (unchanged soft registers) drive the block; block input registers (stage 1), result (stage 2): 2 stages. Same latency, same retiming.

Reset. The block has no clock enable and no bypass, and the original correlator pipeline is free-running with no reset, so the block's reset must be asserted exactly once at power-up (to clear the X the block starts in) and must never clear the block at a moment when the lost values could reach corr_out. Add at the top of sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem:

reg rst_q;
always @(posedge tm3_clk_v0) rst_q <= rst ;
wire sop_rst = rst & rst_q ;

sop_rst is high only on the second and later cycles of a run of rst. Why that is sufficient and necessary: suppose rst is high over cycles [a,b] with b >= a+1. Then sop_rst is high over [a+1,b], the block clears at edges a+2..b+1, and its result differs from the original corr_out_tmp exactly on cycles a+2..b+2. corr_out loads corr_out_tmp at edge t only when new_data(=wen) was high on cycle t-1, and wen is vidin_new_data_scld_{1,2,4}_2to3_left, which port_bus_2to1_1 drives to 0 on every cycle whose previous-cycle counter_out_tmp was not 3'b001 (lines 955-1014), while counter_out_tmp is cleared to 0 by rst (lines 937-944). Hence rst=1 on cycle u forces wen=0 on cycle u+2, which covers precisely cycles a+2..b+2 — every corrupted value is masked, and from cycle b+3 the block tracks the original again. A single-cycle rst never asserts sop_rst, so the block keeps running just as the original correlators do. sop_rst must stay combinational: registering it would shift the clear one cycle later and leave cycle b+3 uncovered. The same argument covers corr_seq, whose in_*_reg are held while wen=0 and so are still correct when the block resumes loading. rst_q is an added register and starts unknown; nothing depends on its initial value — sop_rst is X only until the first edge that samples rst, after which the remaining edges of the start-up reset pulse clear the block, and wen is held at 0 throughout the start-up reset so no X reaches corr_out.

Threading the reset. Add a last input port sop_rst to wrapper_norm_corr_20, wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_norm_corr_10, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_norm_corr_5_seq, wrapper_corr_5_seq, corr and corr_seq. Append , sop_rst to the six positional wrapper_norm_corr_* instantiations at lines 261, 268, 275, 281, 287, 292; add .sop_rst(sop_rst) to the named wrapper_corr_* instantiations at lines 1104, 1491, 1677; append , sop_rst to the positional corr/corr_seq instantiations at lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610 and 1734-1739. Put the new port last everywhere so the existing positional connections are unaffected.

Edits in corr (1352-1387). Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp (1367-1369) and the three assignments at 1374, 1375 and 1385; add wire[36:0] corr_out_tmp_sop; and one instance

int_sop_2 sop_inst (.clk(clk), .reset(sop_rst), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
.bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(corr_out_tmp_sop), .chainout());

leaving only if (new_data) corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; else corr_out <= corr_out; in the always block.

Edits in corr_seq (1829-1871). Same deletions (1844-1846, 1867-1869); keep the in_*_reg assignments and the corr_out assignment exactly as they are, with corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; instantiate the block with ax/ay/bx/by taken from in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg. Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to stereovision1.v; the model is supplied by the framework.

Blocks, tiles and chaining. The rewrite instantiates 76 blocks (64 from corr, 12 from corr_seq), each taking a DSP tile alone, so the design then uses 76 DSP tiles against the baseline's 42, out of 306 on the device. No chaining is used: each correlator's sum has exactly two products and its value must be visible at corr_out, so chainin is tied to 0 and chainout is left unconnected on every block. The longest series of blocks in any stage is therefore 1, and that stage is the block's own input-register-to-result-register path, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.47292 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite leaves. Three new stage shapes, all from the sampled delays on this design: sh_reg flip-flop into a block input register, 0.060 + 1.732 (median register-to-first-block wire) + 0.019 = 1.811 ns; inside the block, 2.219 ns; the block's result register out through the new_data mux into the soft corr_out flip-flop, 0.060 + 0.96 (median wire into soft logic) + 1.14 (median soft level) + 0.019 ≈ 2.18 ns. The slowest of these is 2.219 ns, reported as STAGE_NS. The sop_rst net is one LUT level from rst_q to 76 block reset pins: 0.060 + one soft level + one block-to-block wire + 0.019, roughly 2.4 ns at the median and under 3.5 ns at the long end — still shorter than the baseline critical path; if the editor finds it uncomfortably long, the rst & rst_q AND may be replicated per wrapper, which is a pure duplication of combinational logic and changes no behaviour. Stages the rewrite does not touch (the dividers, wrapper_norm, the two port_bus modules, the top-level 18-bit p+n adders) keep their baseline delays; the clock period after the rewrite is set by the slowest of those, which is below the two paths reported for the baseline since both of those are converted.

Soft logic that leaves, and from which stage. Per correlator: lrexrre_reg[15:0] and limxrim_reg[15:0] (32 flip-flops), the 16-bit adder (about 16 carry-chain cells) and corr_out_tmp[15:0] (16 flip-flops) — about 48 BLEs each, 3648 across the 76 instances. The product flip-flops end the baseline's slowest stage (hard multiply on a DSP tile straight into a flip-flop, 5.47292 ns on path 1 and 5.427 ns on path 2); that stage is the slowest and it disappears completely, 152 instances of it. The adder plus corr_out_tmp form the following stage, which is not the slowest; it disappears too. Nothing soft is added except one flip-flop (rst_q) and one AND gate for the whole design.

Objective. Area: +34 DSP tiles (+8.63e6) against roughly 3648 BLEs of soft logic removed from the 536 clusters the baseline uses (1.496e7 of cluster area), so the used-area term moves by well under 1.3x and plausibly less. Delay: both reported critical paths and all 150 other stages of the same shape are replaced by stages of at most 2.219 ns, so the period is set by soft stages that were previously slack — the dominant gain. Wirelength: the 32 bits per correlator that ran from a DSP output to soft product flip-flops (the 1.45 ns leg of the baseline critical path) and the 16 adder-to-flip-flop nets are gone, 3648 nets in all, against a wider spread over 76 tiles instead of 42. The product of the three is expected well below the baseline's 2.14961e13, driven by the delay term; this is the reason for accepting the DSP tile increase, which the cost guidance warns about.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two changes to the design itself would let the block hold more. First, corr_seq gates its operand registers in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg with new_data (lines 1850-1865); if those registers were free-running like corr's pipeline, they would fold into the block's input registers and 384 more flip-flops would leave soft logic. Second, each correlator's sum has only two products, so a whole DSP tile now carries the two 8x8 multiplies the baseline packed four to a tile; if the top-level pair sum corr_out_1X <= {p[15],p[15],p} + {n[15],n[15],n} (lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405) were moved ahead of the enabled corr_out registers and expressed as an unsigned 16-bit sum with the two sign-extension bits corrected outside, the p and n correlators' four products would fit one chained pair of blocks and the tile count would stay near the baseline's 42 instead of rising to 76.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The design source is stereovision1.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 35 of the 42 tiles.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — one reduction per correlator instance, 76 reductions in all
BLOCKS: 76
TILES: 76
STAGE_NS: 2.219
MAPPING: one int_sop_2 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12).

  • In corr: clk=clk (tm3_clk_v0), reset=sop_rst (new, = rst & rst_q), mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im}, by={11'b0,in_r_im}, chainin=37'b0; result[15:0] becomes corr_out_tmp (the value corr_out samples when new_data), result[36:16] unused, chainout unconnected.
  • In corr_seq: clk=clk, reset=sop_rst, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0,in_r_im_reg}, chainin=37'b0; result[15:0] becomes corr_out_tmp, chainout unconnected. The four in_*_reg stay in soft logic (they have a clock enable, new_data, which the block has no port for).

PLAN:

ASSUMES: rst (top port, line 20) is held high across at least two consecutive clock edges at power-up. Support: rst reaches only port_bus_2to1_1 (line 259) and port_bus_1to0 (line 310); neither corr (1352-1387) nor corr_seq (1829-1871) has any reset, so nothing in the baseline can clear the block's registers out of X except a reset the design supplies at start-up, and the block's reset is driven by rst & rst_q (high only on the second and later cycles of a reset pulse). The reference testbench holds rst for RESET_CYCLES=4 cycles (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v lines 15, 238-239), so the assumption holds there; the rewrite matches the original on every other cycle regardless, including after every one-, two- and three-cycle mid-run reset the testbench applies (lines 198-206) and the random resets (line 185).

Candidate regions — all four converted. Lines 1374/1375 are the two products of corr, lines 1867/1868 the two products of corr_seq; in each module the two products are summed into corr_out_tmp one stage later, which is exactly ax*ay + bx*by. Nothing is left: these are the only four multiply expressions in the file, so the rewrite removes every mult_9x9_fixed_pt from the design. Partial conversion was rejected: the baseline's slowest stage (5.47292 ns) and its second slowest (5.427 ns) are both corr product registers of this same shape, and there are 152 such stages, so converting only some of them would add DSP tiles while leaving the clock period where it is.

Correctness of the arithmetic. in_l_re, in_l_im, in_r_re, in_r_im are plain input[7:0] (lines 1357-1360, 1834-1837), so both products are unsigned 8x8 and exact in 16 bits; the block zero-extends each operand to its port width and the exact sum is at most 2·(2^8-1)^2 = 130050 < 2^17, so no wrap occurs inside the block and result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg (which wraps mod 2^16) bit for bit.

Visibility. lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the sum (1385, 1869); corr_out_tmp is read only by corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1379, 1856). Nothing else in the file reads them, so all three register banks may go inside the block. corr_out itself stays in soft logic because it has the new_data clock enable and is the module's output. The wires in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg declared at 1363-1366 in corr are unused in the baseline; leave them.

Register stages, before and after.

  • corr: before — operands arrive combinationally from the sh_reg outputs, multiply, then lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (stage 1), then the adder, then corr_out_tmp (stage 2): 2 stages from operand to result. After — operands into the block's input registers (stage 1), multiply and add combinationally, result register (stage 2): 2 stages. Same latency; the register moves from after the multiply to before it (this retiming is why the tier is T2, not T1).
  • corr_seq: before — in_*_reg (clock-enabled, kept), multiply, lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (stage 1), adder, corr_out_tmp (stage 2): 2 stages from in_*_reg to result. After — in_*_reg (unchanged soft registers) drive the block; block input registers (stage 1), result (stage 2): 2 stages. Same latency, same retiming.

Reset. The block has no clock enable and no bypass, and the original correlator pipeline is free-running with no reset, so the block's reset must be asserted exactly once at power-up (to clear the X the block starts in) and must never clear the block at a moment when the lost values could reach corr_out. Add at the top of sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem:

reg rst_q;
always @(posedge tm3_clk_v0) rst_q <= rst ;
wire sop_rst = rst & rst_q ;

sop_rst is high only on the second and later cycles of a run of rst. Why that is sufficient and necessary: suppose rst is high over cycles [a,b] with b >= a+1. Then sop_rst is high over [a+1,b], the block clears at edges a+2..b+1, and its result differs from the original corr_out_tmp exactly on cycles a+2..b+2. corr_out loads corr_out_tmp at edge t only when new_data(=wen) was high on cycle t-1, and wen is vidin_new_data_scld_{1,2,4}_2to3_left, which port_bus_2to1_1 drives to 0 on every cycle whose previous-cycle counter_out_tmp was not 3'b001 (lines 955-1014), while counter_out_tmp is cleared to 0 by rst (lines 937-944). Hence rst=1 on cycle u forces wen=0 on cycle u+2, which covers precisely cycles a+2..b+2 — every corrupted value is masked, and from cycle b+3 the block tracks the original again. A single-cycle rst never asserts sop_rst, so the block keeps running just as the original correlators do. sop_rst must stay combinational: registering it would shift the clear one cycle later and leave cycle b+3 uncovered. The same argument covers corr_seq, whose in_*_reg are held while wen=0 and so are still correct when the block resumes loading. rst_q is an added register and starts unknown; nothing depends on its initial value — sop_rst is X only until the first edge that samples rst, after which the remaining edges of the start-up reset pulse clear the block, and wen is held at 0 throughout the start-up reset so no X reaches corr_out.

Threading the reset. Add a last input port sop_rst to wrapper_norm_corr_20, wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_norm_corr_10, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_norm_corr_5_seq, wrapper_corr_5_seq, corr and corr_seq. Append , sop_rst to the six positional wrapper_norm_corr_* instantiations at lines 261, 268, 275, 281, 287, 292; add .sop_rst(sop_rst) to the named wrapper_corr_* instantiations at lines 1104, 1491, 1677; append , sop_rst to the positional corr/corr_seq instantiations at lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610 and 1734-1739. Put the new port last everywhere so the existing positional connections are unaffected.

Edits in corr (1352-1387). Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp (1367-1369) and the three assignments at 1374, 1375 and 1385; add wire[36:0] corr_out_tmp_sop; and one instance

int_sop_2 sop_inst (.clk(clk), .reset(sop_rst), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
.bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(corr_out_tmp_sop), .chainout());

leaving only if (new_data) corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; else corr_out <= corr_out; in the always block.

Edits in corr_seq (1829-1871). Same deletions (1844-1846, 1867-1869); keep the in_*_reg assignments and the corr_out assignment exactly as they are, with corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; instantiate the block with ax/ay/bx/by taken from in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg. Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to stereovision1.v; the model is supplied by the framework.

Blocks, tiles and chaining. The rewrite instantiates 76 blocks (64 from corr, 12 from corr_seq), each taking a DSP tile alone, so the design then uses 76 DSP tiles against the baseline's 42, out of 306 on the device. No chaining is used: each correlator's sum has exactly two products and its value must be visible at corr_out, so chainin is tied to 0 and chainout is left unconnected on every block. The longest series of blocks in any stage is therefore 1, and that stage is the block's own input-register-to-result-register path, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.47292 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite leaves. Three new stage shapes, all from the sampled delays on this design: sh_reg flip-flop into a block input register, 0.060 + 1.732 (median register-to-first-block wire) + 0.019 = 1.811 ns; inside the block, 2.219 ns; the block's result register out through the new_data mux into the soft corr_out flip-flop, 0.060 + 0.96 (median wire into soft logic) + 1.14 (median soft level) + 0.019 ≈ 2.18 ns. The slowest of these is 2.219 ns, reported as STAGE_NS. The sop_rst net is one LUT level from rst_q to 76 block reset pins: 0.060 + one soft level + one block-to-block wire + 0.019, roughly 2.4 ns at the median and under 3.5 ns at the long end — still shorter than the baseline critical path; if the editor finds it uncomfortably long, the rst & rst_q AND may be replicated per wrapper, which is a pure duplication of combinational logic and changes no behaviour. Stages the rewrite does not touch (the dividers, wrapper_norm, the two port_bus modules, the top-level 18-bit p+n adders) keep their baseline delays; the clock period after the rewrite is set by the slowest of those, which is below the two paths reported for the baseline since both of those are converted.

Soft logic that leaves, and from which stage. Per correlator: lrexrre_reg[15:0] and limxrim_reg[15:0] (32 flip-flops), the 16-bit adder (about 16 carry-chain cells) and corr_out_tmp[15:0] (16 flip-flops) — about 48 BLEs each, 3648 across the 76 instances. The product flip-flops end the baseline's slowest stage (hard multiply on a DSP tile straight into a flip-flop, 5.47292 ns on path 1 and 5.427 ns on path 2); that stage is the slowest and it disappears completely, 152 instances of it. The adder plus corr_out_tmp form the following stage, which is not the slowest; it disappears too. Nothing soft is added except one flip-flop (rst_q) and one AND gate for the whole design.

Objective. Area: +34 DSP tiles (+8.63e6) against roughly 3648 BLEs of soft logic removed from the 536 clusters the baseline uses (1.496e7 of cluster area), so the used-area term moves by well under 1.3x and plausibly less. Delay: both reported critical paths and all 150 other stages of the same shape are replaced by stages of at most 2.219 ns, so the period is set by soft stages that were previously slack — the dominant gain. Wirelength: the 32 bits per correlator that ran from a DSP output to soft product flip-flops (the 1.45 ns leg of the baseline critical path) and the 16 adder-to-flip-flop nets are gone, 3648 nets in all, against a wider spread over 76 tiles instead of 42. The product of the three is expected well below the baseline's 2.14961e13, driven by the delay term; this is the reason for accepting the DSP tile increase, which the cost guidance warns about.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two changes to the design itself would let the block hold more. First, corr_seq gates its operand registers in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg with new_data (lines 1850-1865); if those registers were free-running like corr's pipeline, they would fold into the block's input registers and 384 more flip-flops would leave soft logic. Second, each correlator's sum has only two products, so a whole DSP tile now carries the two 8x8 multiplies the baseline packed four to a tile; if the top-level pair sum corr_out_1X <= {p[15],p[15],p} + {n[15],n[15],n} (lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405) were moved ahead of the enabled corr_out registers and expressed as an unsigned 16-bit sum with the two sign-extension bits corrected outside, the p and n correlators' four products would fit one chained pair of blocks and the tile count would stay near the baseline's 42 instead of rising to 76.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision1.v

response

I'll read the design source and the reference files before judging.

APPROVE

Checks performed against stereovision1.v, sim_reference/int_sop_2.v and sim_reference/tb_equiv_sv1_top.v:

  • Terms/arithmetic. corr (stereovision1.v:1374-1375,1385) and corr_seq (stereovision1.v:1867-1869) are exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im, matching ax*ay+bx*by with the instance port order at stereovision1.v:1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739. Operands are [sh_reg_w-1:0] = [7:0] (stereovision1.v:1357-1360, 1834-1837), so each product is exact in 16 bits and the sum ≤ 130050 < 2^17; result[15:0] reproduces the original 16-bit wrap bit for bit. chainin/mode_sigs tied to 0, every input port connected.
  • Visibility. lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are read only at stereovision1.v:1385/1869, corr_out_tmp only at stereovision1.v:1379/1856; corr's in_*_reg wires (stereovision1.v:1363-1366) are indeed undriven and unread. corr_seq's enabled in_*_reg correctly stay outside.
  • Latency. Both before and after, operands reach the result in 2 register stages; the block's operand register substitutes for the product register (retiming across the multiply), and corr_out's new_data mux still samples the pre-edge value, so no race with the block's nonblocking result.
  • Reset. Verified the general form of the plan's argument: result(t) can differ only if a clear occurred at edge t or t-1, i.e. rst(t-2)=1; then counter_out_tmp(t-1)=0 (stereovision1.v:937-944) and all of vidin_new_data_scld_{1,2,4}_2to3_left are 0 at cycle t (stereovision1.v:955-1014, 1009-1011), so no corrupted value is ever loaded into corr_out. The 1-cycle and the back-to-back single-cycle pulses of reset_pattern (tb:201,204) never raise sop_rst, the 2- and 3-cycle pulses (tb:202-203) and random resets (tb:185) are covered. At power-up rst_q may be 0 or X; either way the clear lands at the second edge of the 4-cycle startup reset (tb:15,238-239), and new_data is 0 over the X window, so no X reaches an output.
  • Only four multiplies exist in the file (confirmed by search), so "all 42 baseline DSP tiles converted, 76 after" is self-consistent; all four candidate regions converted with reasons, 76 blocks, longest block series 1 at 2.219 ns, the removed soft logic and its stage, and before/after register stages are all stated.

One inaccuracy, not load-bearing: the ASSUMES support says rst reaches only port_bus_2to1_1 (stereovision1.v:259) and port_bus_1to0 (stereovision1.v:310); it is also read in the top module at stereovision1.v:437, which resets horiz/vert/video_state and the SRAM buffers. Those registers are outside the correlator datapath, so the conclusion the plan draws from it — nothing in the baseline clears the correlator pipeline — still holds.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

prompt
Task

Edit stereovision1.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem). The plan names their shared sum as 'corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — one reduction per correlator instance, 76 reductions in all'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh stereovision1.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — one reduction per correlator instance, 76 reductions in all
BLOCKS: 76
TILES: 76
STAGE_NS: 2.219
MAPPING: one int_sop_2 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12).

  • In corr: clk=clk (tm3_clk_v0), reset=sop_rst (new, = rst & rst_q), mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im}, by={11'b0,in_r_im}, chainin=37'b0; result[15:0] becomes corr_out_tmp (the value corr_out samples when new_data), result[36:16] unused, chainout unconnected.
  • In corr_seq: clk=clk, reset=sop_rst, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0,in_r_im_reg}, chainin=37'b0; result[15:0] becomes corr_out_tmp, chainout unconnected. The four in_*_reg stay in soft logic (they have a clock enable, new_data, which the block has no port for).

PLAN:

ASSUMES: rst (top port, line 20) is held high across at least two consecutive clock edges at power-up. Support: rst reaches only port_bus_2to1_1 (line 259) and port_bus_1to0 (line 310); neither corr (1352-1387) nor corr_seq (1829-1871) has any reset, so nothing in the baseline can clear the block's registers out of X except a reset the design supplies at start-up, and the block's reset is driven by rst & rst_q (high only on the second and later cycles of a reset pulse). The reference testbench holds rst for RESET_CYCLES=4 cycles (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v lines 15, 238-239), so the assumption holds there; the rewrite matches the original on every other cycle regardless, including after every one-, two- and three-cycle mid-run reset the testbench applies (lines 198-206) and the random resets (line 185).

Candidate regions — all four converted. Lines 1374/1375 are the two products of corr, lines 1867/1868 the two products of corr_seq; in each module the two products are summed into corr_out_tmp one stage later, which is exactly ax*ay + bx*by. Nothing is left: these are the only four multiply expressions in the file, so the rewrite removes every mult_9x9_fixed_pt from the design. Partial conversion was rejected: the baseline's slowest stage (5.47292 ns) and its second slowest (5.427 ns) are both corr product registers of this same shape, and there are 152 such stages, so converting only some of them would add DSP tiles while leaving the clock period where it is.

Correctness of the arithmetic. in_l_re, in_l_im, in_r_re, in_r_im are plain input[7:0] (lines 1357-1360, 1834-1837), so both products are unsigned 8x8 and exact in 16 bits; the block zero-extends each operand to its port width and the exact sum is at most 2·(2^8-1)^2 = 130050 < 2^17, so no wrap occurs inside the block and result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg (which wraps mod 2^16) bit for bit.

Visibility. lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the sum (1385, 1869); corr_out_tmp is read only by corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1379, 1856). Nothing else in the file reads them, so all three register banks may go inside the block. corr_out itself stays in soft logic because it has the new_data clock enable and is the module's output. The wires in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg declared at 1363-1366 in corr are unused in the baseline; leave them.

Register stages, before and after.

  • corr: before — operands arrive combinationally from the sh_reg outputs, multiply, then lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (stage 1), then the adder, then corr_out_tmp (stage 2): 2 stages from operand to result. After — operands into the block's input registers (stage 1), multiply and add combinationally, result register (stage 2): 2 stages. Same latency; the register moves from after the multiply to before it (this retiming is why the tier is T2, not T1).
  • corr_seq: before — in_*_reg (clock-enabled, kept), multiply, lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (stage 1), adder, corr_out_tmp (stage 2): 2 stages from in_*_reg to result. After — in_*_reg (unchanged soft registers) drive the block; block input registers (stage 1), result (stage 2): 2 stages. Same latency, same retiming.

Reset. The block has no clock enable and no bypass, and the original correlator pipeline is free-running with no reset, so the block's reset must be asserted exactly once at power-up (to clear the X the block starts in) and must never clear the block at a moment when the lost values could reach corr_out. Add at the top of sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem:

reg rst_q;
always @(posedge tm3_clk_v0) rst_q <= rst ;
wire sop_rst = rst & rst_q ;

sop_rst is high only on the second and later cycles of a run of rst. Why that is sufficient and necessary: suppose rst is high over cycles [a,b] with b >= a+1. Then sop_rst is high over [a+1,b], the block clears at edges a+2..b+1, and its result differs from the original corr_out_tmp exactly on cycles a+2..b+2. corr_out loads corr_out_tmp at edge t only when new_data(=wen) was high on cycle t-1, and wen is vidin_new_data_scld_{1,2,4}_2to3_left, which port_bus_2to1_1 drives to 0 on every cycle whose previous-cycle counter_out_tmp was not 3'b001 (lines 955-1014), while counter_out_tmp is cleared to 0 by rst (lines 937-944). Hence rst=1 on cycle u forces wen=0 on cycle u+2, which covers precisely cycles a+2..b+2 — every corrupted value is masked, and from cycle b+3 the block tracks the original again. A single-cycle rst never asserts sop_rst, so the block keeps running just as the original correlators do. sop_rst must stay combinational: registering it would shift the clear one cycle later and leave cycle b+3 uncovered. The same argument covers corr_seq, whose in_*_reg are held while wen=0 and so are still correct when the block resumes loading. rst_q is an added register and starts unknown; nothing depends on its initial value — sop_rst is X only until the first edge that samples rst, after which the remaining edges of the start-up reset pulse clear the block, and wen is held at 0 throughout the start-up reset so no X reaches corr_out.

Threading the reset. Add a last input port sop_rst to wrapper_norm_corr_20, wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_norm_corr_10, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_norm_corr_5_seq, wrapper_corr_5_seq, corr and corr_seq. Append , sop_rst to the six positional wrapper_norm_corr_* instantiations at lines 261, 268, 275, 281, 287, 292; add .sop_rst(sop_rst) to the named wrapper_corr_* instantiations at lines 1104, 1491, 1677; append , sop_rst to the positional corr/corr_seq instantiations at lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610 and 1734-1739. Put the new port last everywhere so the existing positional connections are unaffected.

Edits in corr (1352-1387). Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp (1367-1369) and the three assignments at 1374, 1375 and 1385; add wire[36:0] corr_out_tmp_sop; and one instance

int_sop_2 sop_inst (.clk(clk), .reset(sop_rst), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
.bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(corr_out_tmp_sop), .chainout());

leaving only if (new_data) corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; else corr_out <= corr_out; in the always block.

Edits in corr_seq (1829-1871). Same deletions (1844-1846, 1867-1869); keep the in_*_reg assignments and the corr_out assignment exactly as they are, with corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; instantiate the block with ax/ay/bx/by taken from in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg. Do not add a definition of int_sop_2 to stereovision1.v; the model is supplied by the framework.

Blocks, tiles and chaining. The rewrite instantiates 76 blocks (64 from corr, 12 from corr_seq), each taking a DSP tile alone, so the design then uses 76 DSP tiles against the baseline's 42, out of 306 on the device. No chaining is used: each correlator's sum has exactly two products and its value must be visible at corr_out, so chainin is tied to 0 and chainout is left unconnected on every block. The longest series of blocks in any stage is therefore 1, and that stage is the block's own input-register-to-result-register path, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.47292 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite leaves. Three new stage shapes, all from the sampled delays on this design: sh_reg flip-flop into a block input register, 0.060 + 1.732 (median register-to-first-block wire) + 0.019 = 1.811 ns; inside the block, 2.219 ns; the block's result register out through the new_data mux into the soft corr_out flip-flop, 0.060 + 0.96 (median wire into soft logic) + 1.14 (median soft level) + 0.019 ≈ 2.18 ns. The slowest of these is 2.219 ns, reported as STAGE_NS. The sop_rst net is one LUT level from rst_q to 76 block reset pins: 0.060 + one soft level + one block-to-block wire + 0.019, roughly 2.4 ns at the median and under 3.5 ns at the long end — still shorter than the baseline critical path; if the editor finds it uncomfortably long, the rst & rst_q AND may be replicated per wrapper, which is a pure duplication of combinational logic and changes no behaviour. Stages the rewrite does not touch (the dividers, wrapper_norm, the two port_bus modules, the top-level 18-bit p+n adders) keep their baseline delays; the clock period after the rewrite is set by the slowest of those, which is below the two paths reported for the baseline since both of those are converted.

Soft logic that leaves, and from which stage. Per correlator: lrexrre_reg[15:0] and limxrim_reg[15:0] (32 flip-flops), the 16-bit adder (about 16 carry-chain cells) and corr_out_tmp[15:0] (16 flip-flops) — about 48 BLEs each, 3648 across the 76 instances. The product flip-flops end the baseline's slowest stage (hard multiply on a DSP tile straight into a flip-flop, 5.47292 ns on path 1 and 5.427 ns on path 2); that stage is the slowest and it disappears completely, 152 instances of it. The adder plus corr_out_tmp form the following stage, which is not the slowest; it disappears too. Nothing soft is added except one flip-flop (rst_q) and one AND gate for the whole design.

Objective. Area: +34 DSP tiles (+8.63e6) against roughly 3648 BLEs of soft logic removed from the 536 clusters the baseline uses (1.496e7 of cluster area), so the used-area term moves by well under 1.3x and plausibly less. Delay: both reported critical paths and all 150 other stages of the same shape are replaced by stages of at most 2.219 ns, so the period is set by soft stages that were previously slack — the dominant gain. Wirelength: the 32 bits per correlator that ran from a DSP output to soft product flip-flops (the 1.45 ns leg of the baseline critical path) and the 16 adder-to-flip-flop nets are gone, 3648 nets in all, against a wider spread over 76 tiles instead of 42. The product of the three is expected well below the baseline's 2.14961e13, driven by the delay term; this is the reason for accepting the DSP tile increase, which the cost guidance warns about.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What I changed, region by region

sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (top, after input tm3_clk_v0) — added the block reset: reg rst_q, always @(posedge tm3_clk_v0) rst_q <= rst;, assign sop_rst = rst & rst_q;. sop_rst is high only on the second and later cycles of a rst pulse, so the cycles on which the blocks are cleared are exactly the cycles on which wen is already 0 (a rst on cycle u forces vidin_new_data_scld_{1,2,4}_2to3_left to 0 on cycle u+2, via counter_out_tmp at lines 937–944/955 of the original numbering), and a one-cycle rst never clears a block — matching the original correlators, which have no reset.

corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 and :1375$79 (module corr) — deleted lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp and the three assignments that formed them, and put the pair of products plus their sum on one int_sop_2: clk=clk, reset=sop_rst, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,in_l_re}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re}, bx={10'b0,in_l_im}, by={11'b0,in_r_im}, chainin=37'b0, chainout unconnected. The always block keeps only the new_data-enabled corr_out <= corr_out_tmp_sop[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]. The operands are 8-bit unsigned (lines 1364–1367), so the exact sum is ≤ 130050 < 2^17 and result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit sum bit for bit. Latency is unchanged at 2 stages; the register moves from after the multiply to before it.

corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104 and :1868$105 (module corr_seq) — same deletions and the same int_sop_2 instance, with ax/ay/bx/by driven from in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg. The four in_*_reg stay in soft logic (they have the new_data clock enable, which the block has no port for), and corr_out keeps its enable.

Reset threading — added sop_rst as the last port of wrapper_norm_corr_20, wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_norm_corr_10, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_norm_corr_5_seq, wrapper_corr_5_seq, corr and corr_seq; appended it to the six positional wrapper_norm_corr_* instances in the top and to the 38 positional corr/corr_seq instances, and added .sop_rst(sop_rst) to the three named wrapper_corr_* instances. Putting the port last leaves every existing positional connection untouched.

ASSUMES: rst (top port, line 20) is held high across at least two consecutive clock edges at power-up, so that sop_rst asserts and clears the blocks out of X. Support: rst reaches only port_bus_2to1_1 and port_bus_1to0; neither corr nor corr_seq has any reset in the baseline, so nothing else can clear the block registers at start-up. The reference testbench holds rst for RESET_CYCLES=4 cycles (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v lines 15, 238–239). On every other cycle, including all mid-run one-, two- and three-cycle resets (lines 198–206) and the random resets (line 185), the rewrite matches the original regardless of the assumption.

No int_sop_2 module definition was added to the design; chainin is tied to 0 and chainout left unconnected on all 76 blocks (no chaining).

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
// result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
// chainin to chainout 1).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module int_sop_2 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
    input [36:0] chainin,
    output reg [36:0] result,
    output [36:0] chainout
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;

    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            result <= 0;
            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
        end
    end

    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers

endmodule
================

module corr (clk, new_data, in_l_re, in_l_im, in_r_re, in_r_im, corr_out);

    parameter sh_reg_w  = 4'b1000;
    input clk; 
    input new_data; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_re; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_im; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_re; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_im; 
    output[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out;
    wire[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_re_reg; 
    wire[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_im_reg; 
    wire[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_re_reg; 
    wire[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_im_reg; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] lrexrre_reg; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] limxrim_reg; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp; 

    always @(posedge clk)
    begin
			// PAJ - edf xilinx files converted to multiply 
			lrexrre_reg <= in_l_re * in_r_re;
			limxrim_reg <= in_l_im * in_r_im;

          if (new_data == 1'b1)
          begin
             corr_out <= corr_out_tmp ; 
          end 
          else
          begin
             corr_out <= corr_out; 
          end 
          corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg ; 
    end 
 endmodule

    always @(posedge clk)
    begin
          if (new_data == 1'b1)
          begin

             in_l_im_reg <= in_l_im ; 
             in_r_re_reg <= in_r_re ; 
             in_r_im_reg <= in_r_im ; 
             corr_out <= corr_out_tmp ; 
          end 
          else
          begin
             in_l_re_reg <= in_l_re_reg ; 
             in_l_im_reg <= in_l_im_reg ; 
             in_r_re_reg <= in_r_re_reg ; 
             in_r_im_reg <= in_r_im_reg ; 
             corr_out <= corr_out; 
          end 
			// PAJ - replaced by me, but called mult_slow

             limxrim_reg <= in_l_im_reg*in_r_im_reg ; 
          corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg ; 
    end 
 endmodule
